...digested and fixed. <note>These are alchemical terms: "digesting" involves gentle heating over time, and "fixing" means making a volatile substance stable so it no longer evaporates when heated.</note> I tell you the truth, that I have revealed the rule of the fire, if you understand Nature. Now it remains to speak of the vessel. It must be a vessel of Nature, and two of them are sufficient. The vessel for the first work must be made round, but in the second work, it should be somewhat less [round/large], a

<margin>Form and shape of the vessel</margin>

glass like a vial or an egg. Above all, however, know that Nature has only one single fire; that it works such diverse things is caused by the distance or the inequality of the places <note>The author suggests that while there is one "Natural Fire," its effects change based on how far it is from the substance or the specific environment it is in.</note>.

<margin>Causes of the differing effects of natural fire</margin>

Likewise, there is only one single vessel of Nature, but for the sake of brevity we use two: also of one kind of matter, but made of two substances. If you now wish to set your mind to create a thing, first consider created things; if you cannot understand these things that lie before your eyes, you will understand much less that which you wish to create and make. For you should know that you can create nothing, as such belongs to God alone.

<margin>To create is the work of God; to advance created things is the work of Art. <note>original: "Creare opus Dei est, creata promovere, Artis." Here, "Art" refers to the Alchemist's skill in assisting nature.</note></margin>

But to make visible hidden things that lie in the shadow, and to remove the shadow from them—such a task is given by God to an understanding <term>Philosopher</term> <note>In this context, a "Philosopher" is a practitioner of alchemy or natural science.</note> through Nature.

<margin>One should not look only at the exterior, but at the interior.</margin>

Consider, I pray, a simple water-cloud; who would ever believe that it had within itself all that the whole world has: hard stones, <term>Salts</term> <note>original: "Salia"</note>, air, earth, and fire? Yet to the eye, it seems only to be simple water. What shall I say of the earth, which has within itself water, fire, salt, and air—and yet one sees before them only simple earth?

40

# March
### Has 31 Days.

The sun rises at 6:00 and sets at 6:00.
The day has 12 hours; the night also has 12 hours. <note>This indicates the Spring Equinox, where day and night are of equal length.</note>

***

In **Aries**, **Taurus**, **Sagittarius**, and **Capricorn**, it is good to plant and sow everything that is intended to grow tall.

In **Aquarius**, it is good to prune trees and to trim grapevines.

In **Pisces**, it is good to graft fruit trees, sow, and plant. <note>Grafting is the practice of joining a shoot from one tree onto the trunk of another so they grow together.</note>

In **Cancer**, **Leo**, and **Virgo**, it is not good to sow or plant. <note>These were traditionally considered "barren" signs in astrological agriculture.</note>

In **Libra**, it is good to sow all types of seeds that one wishes to have bloom beautifully.

In **Scorpio**, it is good to sow and plant everything that should grow within the earth <note>Meaning root vegetables.</note>, such as turnips, carrots, etc.

44 	 # The Twelfth Treatise

If one does not believe the same, then with such a person—as one who contradicts the principles and fundamental axioms—there is no disputing; for the deaf and the mute cannot speak. I have said that the Art <note>The "Art" refers to Alchymia, or alchemy, the study of transmuting base metals into gold.</note> is true. For what advantage should all other things in the world have over the metals? Why should we unjustly exclude these alone from the Creator's general blessing of multiplication, by refusing to acknowledge that they possess a seed? Especially since Holy Scripture testifies that such a blessing was implanted and communicated to all created things from the beginning. 

<margin>The Art is true, Nature is true, but the artist is rarely so.</margin>

But if they possess a seed, who would be so foolish as to be unable to believe that they should be multiplied in their seed? In its very nature, the Chymical Art <note>The alchemical practice</note> is true; Nature is also true; but it is rare that the artist <note>The individual practitioner or alchemist</note> is true and right. As there is one Nature, so there is one Art, but there are many kinds of artists. However, since Nature creates things out of the elements, she generates them through the will of God out of the *prima materia* <note>original: "prima materia"; the First Matter or the fundamental formless substance of the universe</note>, which God alone knows. 

<margin>1. The [First] Matter is known to God alone. The Second [Matter], however, is known to the Philosophers.</margin>

Nature creates things and multiplies them from the "second matter" <note>The physical substance that alchemists work with in the lab</note>, which the Philosophers <note>The "Philosophers" refers to the sages and masters of alchemy</note> know. Nothing happens in the world without the will of God and Nature. For every element is within its own circle, yet one cannot exist without the other; one lives from the other, and yet they are not alike.

70 Dialogue of Nature and Mercury

# Dialogue and Conversation of
# Nature, Mercury, and an
# Alchemist.

<margin>The reason for the writing</margin> At one time, a group of alchemists gathered together and held a general council to discuss how the Philosopher's Stone <note>The legendary goal of alchemy, a substance believed to be capable of turning base metals into gold and granting long life.</note> should be prepared. They decided among themselves that every person present should share their best ideas and opinions. This assembly took place under the open, free sky, upon a heath, and moreover on a very clear day. Many of those present agreed unanimously that quicksilver—or **Mercury**—was the "first matter" <term>First Matter: Known as "materia prima," this is the original, formless substance from which alchemists believed all things were created.</term>; others, however, believed it was Sulfur, while others still proposed something else entirely. 

Nevertheless, they primarily discussed Mercury, especially citing the writings of the ancient Philosophers <note>In this context, "Philosophers" refers to the great alchemical authorities of the past, such as Hermes Trismegistus or Geber.</note>, because those authors identified it as the true matter and the first matter of all metals. Indeed, the Philosophers are known to cry out and shout, "Our Mercury! Our Mercury!" 

<margin>Mercury is the first matter of metals and of the Stone.</margin> While they were arguing and struggling with one another in this way (each of them eagerly awaiting a final resolution to the question), a heavy storm suddenly broke out. There was thunder, lightning, and hail, along with unheard-of winds and downpours. This violent weather scattered the assembly, driving every individual away into foreign lands and provinces.

<summary>A council of alchemists gathers on a heath to debate whether Mercury is the essential "first matter" of the Philosopher's Stone, only to be violently dispersed by a sudden, powerful storm.</summary>
<keywords>Alchemy, Mercury, Philosopher's Stone, First Matter, Alchemical Council, Sulfur, Quicksilver</keywords>

->### Concluding Remarks<-                                     175

...that it rises to the sick person’s mouth and is ejected through the mouth along with the saliva. If the patient died during such a treatment, the <term>mercury (quicksilver)</term><note>The author refers to *Mercurius*, which in alchemy represents the fluid, passive principle of matter.</note> had no way to exit; it thus remained between the teeth, and the corpse became its natural vessel. It remained locked within that vessel for a long time and, through its own <term>sulfur</term><note>In alchemy, 'sulfur' refers to the active, combustible, and transformative "soul" of a substance, not just the chemical element S.</note>, was transformed into gold. This occurred after it was purified by the natural heat of <term>putrefaction</term><note>Decomposition (putrefaction) was considered a vital stage in alchemical transformation, breaking down matter so it could be reborn in a higher form.</note> from the sharp, corrosive slime of the human body. But if no mineral mercury had been placed there, gold would never have grown. 

And this is a true example showing that Nature, in the clefts of the earth, creates gold and silver—as well as other metals—from mercury alone, depending on the condition of the location or the "mother" <note>original: *Muter*. In alchemy, the 'mother' or matrix refers to the specific environment or 'womb' in the earth where minerals are thought to gestate.</note>. Because mercury carries its own sulfur with it and within itself, it is <term>coagulated</term><note>Solidified or hardened from a liquid state into a metal.</note> into gold, provided it is not hindered by some accident, or lacks the proper heat, or a sealed place. 

For this reason, the power of "animal sulfur" <note>The vital heat or life-force found in living creatures.</note> cannot coagulate mercury into gold, but only into flesh; for if such a power existed within humans, it would happen in all bodies, which is not the case. Likewise, many miracles and wonders occur which, if not well-considered by writers, lead readers into error. But a sensible researcher should judge everything according to the possibilities of Nature: so it is with...

<summary>The author uses a macabre anecdote about mercury turning into gold inside a deceased patient's mouth to argue that metals grow naturally from mercury and internal sulfur. He distinguishes between the "animal sulfur" of living beings and the "mineral sulfur" required for metallic transmutation.</summary>

<margin>Sulfur is the coagulant of Mercury. <note>original: "Sulphur est Mercurii Coagulum."</note></margin>

Sulfur, however, is the hardening, or the <term>rennet</term><note>The original uses "lipp" (modern German "Lab"), the enzyme used to curdle milk into cheese. In alchemy, sulfur was thought to "curdle" or solidify fluid mercury into metal.</note> of Mercury: which Sulfur is indeed prepared with great
difficulty, and found even more sparingly; for in the
<term>Sulfur of the Wise</term><note>Also called "Philosophers' Sulfur," this is not common brimstone but a purified alchemical principle.</note> lies the entire secret,
which is also found in the innermost part of Mercury; 
concerning whose preparation (without which it is useless) 
we intend to treat shortly in the third treatise, concerning 
Salt; but here we speak of the origin and the power 
of Sulfur.

[Paragraph ornament] Let a diligent student of this art be satisfied 
that he has understood the source and origin of the 
principles in this place; for when one does not know 
the beginning, the end is always doubtful: of 
which we [speak] in this treatise not in riddles,

## A harmonious conversation held between the Spirit of Mercury and a Monastic Philosopher.

->This has been added here because of the similarity of the subject matter, and to complete this little treatise, having been taken from an old book.<-

<note>original: "Est in Mercurio quicquid quærunt Sapientes."</note> **Everything that the Wise seek is found within Mercury.**

Within me, Mercury, all is concealed,
For which the alchemists have toiled and appealed.
I carry within me both water and fire,
I am also earth and wind, vast and dire.
And I possess within me <term>Sulfur</term><note>In alchemy, Sulfur represents the "soul" of a substance and its ability to burn or transform.</note> and <term>Salt</term><note>Salt represents the "body" or the physical, solid remains of a substance after transformation.</note>;
If you find it, keep it secret in your vault.

178 Conversation of the Spirit Mercury

# Conversation of the Spirit Mercury / With Brother Albert
# Bayr / Carmelite
# Monk. etc.

### Mercury.

[Decorative initial W]hat is the reason / that you have enchanted and banished <note>In the context of 16th and 17th-century magic, "banished" (gebannt) often referred to "binding" a spirit or forcing it to appear and stay within a specific circle or vessel.</note> me with so much idolatry and incantations?

### Albert.

I will tell you the reason / if you will first assure me against the danger to my body and life / and to my soul.

### Mercury.

That does not stand within my power / but I have not come / to do such a thing to you: however, if you do not desist from your sorcery / you are already commended to another / who will well know how to carry out the execution <note>Likely referring to a demonic punisher or the "execution" of divine justice.</note> upon you and your kind. Regarding the salvation of your soul / I can neither hinder nor promote you / but if I were a human / I would certainly wish to be saved / therefore answer me my question.

### Albert.

I beg of you / do not be angry with me / for I am a timid <note>The original term "blöder" in Early Modern German meant "weak," "timid," or "frail" rather than the modern meaning of "stupid."</note> man / but you are a swift spirit / and powerful / therefore tell me first / whether you are a good or an evil angel / or who you are?

Mercury.

With Brother Alberto Bahrs.

->Whether what the Philosophers have written of their **Stone of the Philosophers**, or **Tincture**, is truly found in the nature of things <note>original: "in rerum natura"</note>, or if it is merely a subtle speculation?<-

**Mercurius.**

Know that the Philosophers have written regarding this one thing with such caution and variety so that the fools—who only ask and strive after money—and unfaithful, prideful men might be led astray. In this way, the high secrets of Nature (which are the natural forces that accomplish everything) may remain all the more secret, though many people of both high and low station strive after them. But the Philosophers have not told, nor can they tell, the truth in any other way except through this one single thing (which is all in all); everything else serves more to mislead the unworthy. Therefore, I tell you in brief words the pure truth: when they have written in harmony <note>original: "Concordantz"</note> regarding their **Stone** or **Tincture**, that is true and certain in the nature of things.

**Albertus.**

What is that same single thing?

**Mercurius.**

Since you are a well-read <term>Sophist</term> <note>In this context, a "sophist" refers to someone trained in formal logic and academic philosophy.</note> and a practiced <term>Laborant</term> <note>original: "Laborant", referring to a practical chemist or alchemical worker who performs experiments "at the labor," or the furnace.</note>, you should at least have learned from your Bernard <note>Refers to Bernard of Trevisan, a famous 15th-century alchemical author often cited in these dialogues.</note>—as you imagine yourself to be—since you believe you know his double **Spirit of Mercury**.

182 ->Dialogue of the Spirit of Mercury<-

...quite well, and you have speculated yourself almost into a fool over the <term>First Being<note>original: "primo Ente"; refers to the primary, unformed matter that alchemists believed was the source of all things.</note></term> and your <term>Azoth<note>A term for the "universal solvent" or the essential creative spirit of nature, often symbolized by the planet Mercury.</note></term>; yet you are still very far from the true Center, in that you seek life among the dead. You seek the most constant and indestructible strength—of all natural strength, the strongest strength—within inconstant and destructible things.

Therefore, know in truth that our <term>tincture<note>A potent alchemical substance capable of transmuting base metals into gold or healing the human body.</note></term> becomes entirely red and pure, extracted from the most perfect creature that the sun has ever shone upon. This single thing is so compactly joined together through the most constant spirits, the composition of the four different Qualities or Elements, and the Harmony of the <term>seven stars<note>Refers to the seven wandering celestial bodies of the ancient world (Sun, Moon, Mercury, Venus, Mars, Jupiter, Saturn), which corresponded to the seven sacred metals.</note></term>. 

It is perfected and brought into its <term>degree of perfection<note>original: "gradu perfectionis"</note></term> without any human addition, help, or art. Furthermore, it is so naturally gifted in its creation with an incredible multiplication of its own seed and offspring that its parts are firmly bound together; thus, it cannot be naturally destroyed or harmed by any element without help or art. 

While outside of this one thing, all other natural things are otherwise subject to <term>corruption<note>In this context, decay or the breaking down of physical matter.</note></term>. Let this be enough report for you this time regarding what kind of matter the Philosophers have drawn their tinctures from. 

**Note:** If you understand or recognize what is contained in these chosen words, then you understand the entire business and the <term>Sum of the Art<note>original: "Summam der kunst"; the total essence of alchemical knowledge.</note></term>. Indeed, to him whose eyes God opens, enough has been said here.

The text emphasizes that the secret of alchemy lies in understanding this specific, harmonious "creature" rather than speculating on lesser, perishable materials.</summary>

## With Brother Albertus Bayr.

...said. It might also be interpreted as referring to gold, but it is not understood correctly; for there are even higher created beings than gold, which must now be sought out, so that the truth of what God has placed in Nature might be found—which man does not wish to recognize, even if it were written right under his nose; it is therefore not to be grasped because of his great blindness and lack of self-knowledge.

**Albertus.**

I understand from your obscure report that you mean fine gold.

**Mercurius.**

You have understood in part, but a dark cloud still floats before your eyes. It is the finest gold, but not the kind that will be found in the smelting furnace, but that which Nature itself has refined through its **Volcanic Archeus** <note>In Paracelsian alchemy, the 'Archeus' is the "inner chemist" or vital force of nature that directs the growth and transformation of all things.</note> without any help from <term>art</term> <note>In this context, 'art' refers to human intervention or laboratory technology as opposed to natural processes.</note>, in its own way. Azoth was considered the universal solvent, the "Alpha and Omega," or the essential secret of the alchemical Work.</note> **NB.** <note>original: "Nota Bene" or "Note Well."</note> It is obvious that it is more than the finest gold, which God himself commanded in the creation and granted it this power to reveal such things to us humans. Thereby all people can possess this, if they are rightly enlightened by God.

Yes, where does one then obtain this gold?

**Mercury.**

Under the heavens, in many mountains and mines. <note>NB: All people have it before their eyes and do not recognize it.</note>

**Albertus.**

How much must one have to complete this work?

**Mercury.**

If you have 4 Loth <note>A 'Loth' was a pre-metric unit of mass used in German-speaking lands, roughly equivalent to 15 grams or half an ounce.</note>, you could buy the crowns from the Pope and keep the rest.

**Albertus.**

That much we shall certainly manage to obtain with God's help. <note>NB: If you have worked out 4 loth, it is enough for your beginning.</note>

**Mercury.**

Yes, the body <note>original: "Corpus"</note>. But do you not know that I, as a spirit, speak not of the body but much more of the spirit <note>original: "Spiritu"</note>? ...transform the body, then you may debate with your Abbot (though beforehand it is unnecessary) and say: Fire and Azoth <note>original: "ignis et Azoth tibi sufficiunt." Azoth was considered by alchemists to be the universal solvent or the "first matter" of all metals.</note> are sufficient for you.

**Albertus.**

Oh, angelic, oh heavenly words! How then shall I do it?

**Mercurius.**

Dissolve and coagulate. <margin>that is: coagulate, dissolve, and coagulate</margin>

**Albertus.**

Oh, those are short words that are difficult to understand! But the whole Art is contained within them. I understand: I should dissolve the body of the sun <note>original: "corpus solis." In alchemical theory, Sol or the Sun represents gold or the perfected masculine principle.</note> and through that dissolution draw out the tinging spirit <note>original: "spiritum tingentem," the active essence capable of transforming or "dyeing" a base metal into gold.</note>, which without doubt is the "double Mercury" of Bernhardus <note>Likely referring to Bernard of Trevisan, a 15th-century alchemist who wrote extensively on the "mercurial" nature of the Stone.</note>. **NB.** <note>Latin abbreviation for "Nota Bene" or "Note Well."</note> The "body" is not fine gold, but rather that in which the tincture lies hidden; from that, draw the double Mercury.

Now the veil is partly removed from your eyes; you have understood it correctly. **NB.** now understand which body he means.

**Albertus.**

By what means must I dissolve the body of the sun?

**Mercurius.**

Through itself, and that which is most closely related to it.

With Brother Albertus Bayr. 187

...later all will be revealed which you do not now understand. Yes, you will complete it, but your Abbot will not live that long. You will obtain it after his death; and if you do not take wise precautions, it will be the cause of your own death. Yet you should guard your little books more diligently at all times than even the tincture itself, and take care that they are never found in your possession. ->**Conversation of the Spirit of Mercury**<-

[To] **God** and his **Angels**, and dear **Saints**,
that in the year 1568, on the 18th day of February—which was the feast of the illustrious and highly blessed, ever-eternal **Virgin Mary’s Candlemas** <note>original: "Liechtmeß." While Candlemas is traditionally February 2nd, the author specifically links his vision on the 18th to this feast of purification.</note>—while I was in my cell in the monastery of *Maria Magdalena of the New Star* <note>original: "Maria Magdalena de stella nova"</note>, such a vision appeared to me and held the aforementioned conversation with me. After I had risen and gone to bed day and night with <term>philosophical books</term> <note>In the 16th century, "philosophy" specifically referred to "natural philosophy" or alchemy.</note> and thoughts, and prayed to **God the Lord** with fervent sighs day and night that He would graciously reveal the truth of this **Art** <note>The "Art" refers to the Great Work of alchemy: the transmutation of base metals into gold or the creation of the Philosopher's Stone.</note> to me. 

Then, in my ignorance—**God** forgive me—because I believed nothing else, after I had worked in vain with great effort alongside my Abbot for 23 years, and diligently tended the **fire** day and night, [I thought] that the secret of these matters could be learned from no man, but rather that one must force it from the spirits. Yet, it is far more possible for men than for spirits, as I—praise **God**—found in the end. 

Thus, on the mentioned day, through the customary ceremonies and proper **conjurations** <note>original: "beschwerungen," referring to ritual magic used to compel spirits to appear.</note>—as is quite common in monasteries in Italy and Spain—acting as a monastic **exorcist**—**God** forgive me—I summoned the **spirit of Mercury** <note>original: "spiritum Mercurij." Mercury was both a Roman god and the alchemical name for quicksilver, representing the volatile principle of nature.</note> and called him to a conversation. He appeared to me in the form of a black, elongated, hideous glow or shadow, without any form or shape of a human or animal, and with a hollow—

<summary>A monk recounts how, after twenty-three years of fruitless alchemical labor, he used his authority as a monastic exorcist to summon the Spirit of Mercury to reveal the secrets of the "Art."</summary>

->With Brother Albertus Bayer’s. • 189<-

...with a resounding, ringing voice, gave question and answer, as previously reported. And when I sat at the table at his command, ready with pen and ink, the same shadow or black radiance stepped into the middle of the circle, regardless of the consecrated sword, the smoked candles, and other jugglery. <note>The author uses 'gauckelwerck' (jugglery/magic tricks) to describe ritual tools, perhaps suggesting a distinction between outward ceremonies and the actual spiritual manifestation.</note> Afterwards, it changed from the black color through ash-colored gray clouds into an entirely bright white radiance, and finally was changed from the white through a light-yellow color into the highest redness. <note>These colors—black, white, and red—correspond to the three main stages of the alchemical 'Great Work': Nigredo, Albedo, and Rubedo.</note> The form and size, however, did not shift or change, but remained completely unmoved in the circle until the end of the conversation; but in the middle of the radiance stood the sign of Mercury in three different colors.

Finally, when he disappeared as recounted, the inside and outside of my cell appeared and was seen as blood-red, just as the sun is wont to shine blood-red into a room. After this revelation, I arranged everything with my Abbot; within two years, we produced 11 pounds and 7 <term>loth</term> <note>A traditional Germanic unit of weight; a loth was roughly equivalent to 15-16 grams or half an ounce.</note> of the right <term>Matter</term> <note>original: "Matery"—referring to the raw material required for alchemical transmutation.</note> with great effort and diligence, and successfully completed the <term>Work</term> <note>The 'Great Work' or Magnum Opus, the successful creation of the Philosopher's Stone.</note> in the year 1571, as I have then faithfully and clearly recorded in the following pages. But my Abbot did not live to see it, for on the 2nd of June prior, he was found dead in bed next to his concubine. I saw all the colors from beginning to end, and just as the <term>Spirit</term> <note>original: "Spiritus"</note> manifested in the circle, so also the three main colors, namely, black...

<summary>The author describes a spirit appearing in a magic circle, changing through the symbolic colors of alchemy to reveal the secrets of the "Great Work." Following this vision, the author successfully completes the alchemical process in 1571, shortly after his Abbot's scandalous death.</summary>

<keywords>Alchemy, Necromancy, Spiritus, Sign of Mercury, Great Work, 1571, Nigredo, Albedo, Rubedo, Brother Albertus Bayer</keywords>

130 Conversation of the Spirit Mercury with Brother Albert of Bavaria.
black, white, and red <note>These represent the three main stages of the alchemical "Great Work": nigredo (blackening), albedo (whitening), and rubedo (reddening).</note>, found so distinctly in the work; and whenever any single error occurred, I always received good advice and instruction from the aforementioned spirit <note>original: "spiritu"</note>. 
Specifically, however, he explained the parable of the Fountain of Bernard <note>Bernhardus Trevisanus (1406–1490) was a famous Italian alchemist. His "Parable of the Fountain" is a classic allegorical text describing the chemical dissolution and rebirth of matter.</note> to me in such a way that afterwards all the hidden writings and figures of the Chaldeans, Egyptians, and other philosophers became revealed to me—as I have recorded, mostly with my own blood, in the fourth part of my little book which I wrote about the book of Bernard. But after the completion of the work, I could not bring the Spirit <note>original: "Spiritum"</note> back to me for several years. 
Consequently, the multiplication <note>original: "augmentation"; the technical process of increasing the quantity and potency of the alchemical Philosopher’s Stone once it has been created.</note> of the powers and quantities became very difficult for me; and because I received no further instruction or report from the Spirit, and the other brothers—and especially the new abbot—were very hostile and opposed to me because they could learn nothing from me regarding these matters, I slipped away secretly with my tincture <note>A "tincture" in alchemy is a refined medicinal or transmutative liquid, often the goal of the work.</note> and several old and good Egyptian books after a few years. 
I arrived safely in Augsburg and then traveled to Nuremberg, being glad that I had finally reached German soil in the comforting hope that I would one day find someone who would show me the multiplication. 
May God the Almighty further help all with His grace, 
->highly lauded and praised in<- 
->all eternity, Amen Amen<- 
->Amen<-

->**Physical Description of the Manuscript**<-

The image displays the fore-edge of the closed volume. The edges of the paper have been treated with a deep red pigment.

<note>Red-staining on the edges of books (the "fore-edge") was a common practice in early modern bookbinding. This served two purposes: it was decorative, adding a sense of prestige to the volume, and functional, as the pigment helped protect the paper from dust, moisture, and light damage while the book sat on a shelf.</note>
